Lightness: Is that legal?]] – Yes, that’s quite legal, I’m allowed to make it here. Don't be afraid! For centuries hemp plants have been used to produce ropes, paper, textiles, but they also have more than a hundred different ingredients. One is psychoactive – that's THC – the other very little psychoactive, because it's not quite extractable, that's cannabidiol. These two substances are processed into medications, to medicines approved and available in Austria. This raises the first important question: Are medicines that contain cannabinoids, i.e. ingredients of the cannabis plant, available in Austria? – The answer to this is: Yes, in the pharmacy, after prescription of a doctor with a prescription. He can prescribe them as tablets, he can prescribe them as drops, and he can also prescribe individual ingredients on a prescription individually for the patient. Liberalisation for medical purposes is not necessary because these drugs are available. The second question we have to ask ourselves is: What are the benefits of these drugs to other medicines in use? . On the initiative of the Minister who provided us with a report on this, there was an opinion from the Supreme Council of Sanity, which was, of course, attached to the report and to be read. What kind of body is this Supreme Council of Sanity? – The doctor’s chamber, the pharmacist’s chamber, is also represented in it, which is currently the only one in Austria to grow hemp plants with a certain THC content, which is not harmful, and in it is also the society for anaesthesia. What did they say? If we need these drugs, do we need cannabinoids, yes or no? – The answer was as follows: The medications for pain therapy, for increased appetite or for suppressing nausea and vomiting or in the event of pronounced spasms are justified if the other medicines currently on the market are not found or if you have an effect that is better than the medicines we currently have on the market. – That means: No patient gets this drug if he needs it. This makes it clear whether it is clear when these drugs are used. Of course, there is still clinical research in this area, because it is still necessary to improve the evidence situation; and for cannabidiol, i.e. the non-intoxicating part of this plant, no recommendation has been made at present, but one may use it in the case of epilepsy in children if there is nothing more. We are thus on the same line with the German Bundestag, which last week decided that the authorisation requirement – contrary to what the Group of the Left has requested there – will not be abolished for the time being that the authorisation requirement for these drugs remains in Germany. The third question will be: Is this medicine available for all in Austria? Can anyone in Austria take this on equal terms? - That's what we're working on. There is a request from us to evaluate the conditions in which cannabinoids and cannabinoids are available in the various federal states. After completion of this report, this will be available in the same way throughout Austria. Ladies and gentlemen, let us stop with one thing that constantly affects me in politics: with the immeasurable exaggerations and with the unfair game distorted emotions! We are in favour that on a good evidence, in the context of a good evidence the cannabinoid-containing drugs in Austria are available to all patients on the same conditions when they need them.

Especially in rural areas, there is always no general physician for the community, no pediatricians on checkout throughout the region, and no or very few female doctors are available on checkout. If you look at this, you can see that the number of cash-contract doctors has decreased by 3 percent since 2006, while the population has naturally grown. Only the number of electors to whom patients must go if they do not have an appointment with a cashier has increased. You have to ask yourself why this is so. The policy has handed over the job planning for the cash offices of the so-called self-management of the cash offices, and the cash desk officials are dealing with this with the doctor's chamber. There is now a business taking place at the expense of third parties, the cash registers and the doctor's chamber make it clear that there are few cash offices, and both benefit from it. How does that work? If there are fewer checkout offices, of course the checkout benefits, because then people can't go to the cashier's office, the checkouts don't have to pay it, they have to pay a maximum of 80 percent of the cash rate. So for the checkouts, it's always a business if the patient doesn't go to the cash doctor, they have an interest to have few cash offices. But also the doctor's chamber has a nice interest. How does it work on this page? – If there are few checkout offices, the checkout doctors who exist always have the cabin full, there is an e-card after the other. So you can earn a bit with the modest fees paid by the cash registers. The others who work in the electoral field also do a business, because people have to get there, because the cash medical care is not enough. The patients who don't get an appointment from the cashier do this. There are many regions in which this is an issue. For example, in Kufstein district, there is a female doctor on checkout for the entire district. I could list you a lot of districts where you're waiting for an ophthalmologist four or five months if you want to have that at the cashier's office. If, of course, people have to wait four or five months for such an appointment, they will be more willing to put something on it privately. This is the business model, as the checkouts and the doctor's board charged the insured to conclude their contracts. That's why my request is that if the cash medical care is not sufficient, the patient, the insured can go to the election doctor and the cashier must take over. That would cause the cashier to think about it: Oh, what do we have to do that we don't run away from it? How can we ensure cash medical care in such a way that we don't get such medical bills? I have been told in committee that: This is totally missing, then all the electorate would become. That's from the old clothes collection. This can only happen if the patient can avoid it. If I am always helpless of this compulsory insurance, which I cannot choose, then they have me in hand, can hold me short and push me to the election doctor. In reality, this was already decided in the 2013 Amending Act and with effect from 31 December 2013. January 2013 removed, but it has never been formally implemented in the salary law, so to speak. Now it's finally happening. It is often surprising that things have not been executed by a previous government for so long. That's what we're doing now. When it comes to resolving the consequences of an IT defect, which is a technical defect, the Committee has explained very clearly to the officials responsible for it, I believe. What's this about? – In the course of the legal implementation of the Federal Service's salary decision, after completion of the salary negotiations and just in time for the decision to be taken in Parliament, about 5,000 salary approaches must be evaluated in the service law. Dese Valorization is carried out according to an automated system via the IT area. In doing so, an existing sentence was deleted in the legal text due to a harmful software, which should not have been lost. Let's fix that now, that's exactly the background. About 18.4 percent of the Austrian population live with one or more disabilities. These are people living alone, people living in housing communities living in institutions or living with their parents. The care allowance is designed to ensure that these people receive, at least financially, the additional financial burden they have due to their mental or physical health status. This additional effort is different for different people: Older people need assistance other than young people and people with disabilities have other needs than the sick. This needs to be clarified and promoted individually. A carrier-independent care service would be exactly such a turntable that could act as a point of contact. People with disabilities have the same right as everyone and anyone else to choose freely as, where and with whom they want to live. This is often only possible with special support and often only if the necessary small money is available. In order to enable them to have a self-determined life without financial worries, it needs clear and transparent funding. We're talking about people who have little or little income; we're talking about people who are mostly excluded from much of what is natural to others; we're talking about people who know what it means to get out with little; and we're talking about people who can and want to do valuable things for society, if you just leave them – we're talking about people who can't afford a care insurance at all. For us it is absolutely clear that there can be no care insurance, for us it is clear that there can be no additional burden for these people, and for us it is also quite clear that it will only be done with a state-funded care system. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] People with disabilities do valuable things in society. Yes, and they need our support. As politicians, it is our duty to create framework conditions for all to enable real participation in all areas of social life. A uniform national care system that regulates the quality and requirements of all people in Austria would be an important relief for people with disabilities in particular. These demands that I have spoken about are different from those of people with disabilities. They must also have room. You must have room in this discussion and in a sustainable care concept. We need a guarantee for their personal assistance in the field of leisure, which is needs-oriented and uniformly regulated throughout the country. We need uniform national support for technical assistance, not only in school or in work, but also in leisure. We need special training for people in care who will take care of people with disabilities in the retirement centres. We need an annual valorization of the care allowance at all stages of care. And we need better pay and better working conditions for the nurses. Mrs Minister, please stand by Page 119 of the Government Programme, which reads as follows: “With a clear commitment to tax financing from a single source, it must be guaranteed that the money comes to people”. In order to improve this, the caregivers, the care staff, will also be able to carry out tasks that are now carried out by doctors. You'll have to pay them better. In order to do this, it will be necessary to create a maintenance cost catalogue. It's missing now. There is a fee for medical services in a primary supply network, but not yet for the services of the care staff. But that is urgently necessary. That's where we have to go, and I think that's where everyone's called for: We need all good ideas from all sides, no matter who they come from. Let's take a stand for this, let's deal with it! We have the system that, although the federal government provides cash benefits, countries pay benefits in kind and there are mixings. - That's a problem. The second problem is that countries replace costs and set home contributions. However, these amounts are essentially not based on actual costs, but are a negotiation result between the country and the nursing home operator. At the same time, the problem is that the costs are not transparent and are not assured that the money spent actually comes to those concerned – a situation which at the time, when the care fund was introduced, has also led to the attempt to set up care keys, room sizes and quality criteria, so that the stakeholders who have paid more than one billion euros in the past also come to their verifiable benefits. I think we all agree that we would like to provide mobile and non-stop care to every person concerned in a home, which costs more than 30 000 euros per year than mobile care. However, this requires a summary planning and a certain quality to be determined, but at the moment it does not exist. That is to say, there is a system that you, Mrs Klubobfrau, have mentioned, which has been introduced, but at the same time it has formed in the past a structure that does not have the transparency needed to ensure that the needy person also comes to the performance he needs. That is why this reform is now taking place with the involvement of all, including with the involvement of the countries. We will also deal with this in the federal working group. That's our job, that's our responsibility in the field. But before we talk again about how we distribute the money and increase the funds, we have the responsibility that the money – currently more than 6 billion euros a year – is actually used in such a way that it comes to those affected. This requires a comprehensive planning. This also requires that we set quality standards, which has not been the case until now. Finally, it also requires greater transparency in care tariffs. I do not think it is feasible that there are currently more than 200 tariffs in a federal country alone at the same level of care and care. Accordingly, the cost difference is also more than 600 euros. If you want to see what performance you get for, you can't see that. One must also accept this topic with regard to benchmarking: A mobile hour costs 12 euros in a federal state and 30 euros in another federal state. That is to say, one should lift the potential that results not only in order to make the care of the needy affluent as far as the public hand is concerned, but also in order to provide a quality that comes to those concerned. That's exactly the way we're going. That is exactly the way in which all of us are involved, and it is excellent that – this has been addressed – all political groups are ready to take this path because, in the last consequence, it is a way that affects us all. Thank you.

Point Report by the Constitutional Committee on Amendment 603/A by Mr Mag. Wolfgang Gerstl, Mag. Harald Stefan, colleagues in the Federal Law amending the 1996 State Printing Act (546, B.C.)
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e22046	We're on the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Christoph Stark is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e22076	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen in the House! Dear fellow citizens! I am now allowed to come to a much simpler matter than that of care, namely the matter of the State Printing Act, and here I assume unanimity which I can refer to as such and also announce. We are dealing with, as has been said, the State Printing Act which eliminates the Manko in terms of EU competition law. The Austrian state printing company was founded in 1804 as a k. k. court and state printerey, split as a GmbH in 1999 and finally privatized in 2000. Since 2006 everyone – everyone! – Austrian security documents in the high security area of the Austrian state printing company personalised with the data of our citizens and sent to the desired addresses. There will also be safety products of other kinds, namely stamps and other, for customers and Customers on four continents – almost for the whole world – produced. Since November 2011, the Austrian state printing company has also been listed on the Vienna Stock Exchange. The balance sheet of state printing can also be seen: Sales increased, for example, from EUR 40 million in 2015 to EUR 46.7 million in 2017/18, as well as the export rate from 7.3 to 10.7 percent, so this is also a very successful story, which above all the 148 employees write. It is thus a prime example of how one can make a market leader and more, even an innovation leader from a state company. At that time – 2000 – those responsible for privatisation were quite trained. Today, the Austrian state printing company is a solid company and a high-tech presentation company. Customers on four continents also show that they rely on Austrian quality abroad, and the number of products in different areas also underlines the company's innovations. State printing is also on the rise in the digital field: In 2006 the e-pass was introduced, and in the digital ID system, the MIA app, documents such as driving licence and other documents can be displayed on the smartphone. Of course, there is also the possibility of choice between the digital world and the analogue world. In addition to that, security is a very, very important pillar of state printing. This is an important partner for Austria and its customers around the world. Especially in this area, the data is a very high and important asset that must be best protected. It is therefore essential that the point of security be included in all procurement contracts. At this point, as I said, I would like to thank the employees of the state printing company very much for their qualitative and prudent working methods, which are just being appreciated worldwide. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] All customers, for example, who hold an Austrian passport in their hands, appreciate the value of this work as well. Given the international demand for the products, we can rightly be proud of this Austrian company. On the basis of a judgment of the European Court of Justice of March 2018, which called for a Europe-wide tender for services, the existing Austrian system would now be contrary to the freedom to provide services, second, to the Directive on the coordination of procedures for the award of public service contracts and thirdly to the Directive on the coordination of procedures for the award of public supply contracts. Austria has thus violated EU law by maintaining national rules whereby contracting authorities had to award certain service contracts – the so-called security pressure – to state printing. In accordance with the explanatory notes, emergency passports, residence permits and other printing works are also concerned. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that we do not wish to oppose free competition. It is also clear that we appreciate the achievements and quality, the history and the international standing of this highly specialized unit of Austrian state printing. It is all the more gratifying that all parties have joined the new State Printing Act. This draft was adopted unanimously by the Committee. With We naturally comply with the ECJ decision, we have made such changes and we have taken the necessary steps. I now also hope here in plenary that this draft State Printing Act will be adopted unanimously and I look forward to this vote with pleasure. Thank you.

Wolfgang Gerstl, Mag. Harald Stefan, colleagues in a federal law amending the Administrative Court Law 1985 (547 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e22419	We're on the 11th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First word message: Mr Mag. Josef Lettenbichler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e22450	Madam President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We are negotiating at this item on the agenda a simplification in the invitation to tender in the field of the Administrative Court. What is it about, and why are we going to do this? – The trigger for this novel is that in 2019, 2020 and 2021 several new occupations in the administrative court are due to temporary retirement shifts. The question is: What do we do?, because the previous legal regulation provides that the call for re-casting of these plantations must be made at the earliest three months or a month after the date of release of the plant site. Maintaining this system would mean that in the next few years one would actually have to carry out almost permanent tendering procedures, because there are an above average number of new occupations. All of this, of course, is linked to not insignificant effort. Resources are bound, and this Woman and Manpower – in which we all agree – is better used in carrying out the original tasks. In addition, there is a considerable time pressure during the summer months, as decisions in the Council of Ministers are necessary for this entire occupation process, and there are obviously fewer Council of Ministers meetings in summer. So there, too, you would have increased pressure and would at least be in arrears. So, what are we doing? – We now allow several tender procedures to be carried out in one by extending the time-limits for the post-shipping notices by a total of five months – in addition three months before and two months after that. As a result, a string list can be drawn up in the future and, if necessary, a list can be used to refer to it, which or the next one will be used. This is a practice that has proved itself well in many other institutions, and so we want to shape it here too. This is a sign of good cooperation between the Supreme Courts and the Federal Government. The highest courts have approached the government, the legislator, to implement this improvement, this simplification so that we can accelerate. I would also like to thank the opposition parties who support this request. It is an area that perhaps does not cause such a great deal of concern outside the population, in the economy, but it is also a small step towards reform. It may not be so noticeable, but it is also about de-bureaucratisation, and the circle is closing, because our Federal Government and we, as MEPs, mainly the coalition parties, have set us the goal of reforming Austria in many areas and making it fit again. I can express my express thanks to Federal Minister Moser for being the driving force in many, many areas. I should just like to mention his last prelude to Gold Plating, so that we want to cut down many regulations in many areas and avoid overbureaucratisation, especially in the future, in the implementation of EU regulations in Austria. I would also like to mention – because it was recently presented – the initiative of the Federal Government concerning the Digital Office, implemented and implemented by Federal Minister Schramböck. This makes the citizens feel that things are going faster, easier and more cost-effective. Yesterday, the latest initiative was presented, which will also bring about a speedy recovery – in the truest sense of the word – of an initiative for faster Internet implementation, which is now being implemented by T-Mobile. In the area of the 5G network, we have the lead, the pioneering role in Europe. I am also pleased that of the 17 municipalities selected, there are four locations in Tyrol, namely St. Johann in my constituency, Innsbruck, the capital of the state, Serfaus and my hometown Kirchbichl. It is important that we create alternatives to broadband development, which is often associated with very high costs and is sometimes not always the fastest method. It is important that we find in this 5G area a fast data-high-light motorway. The rollout is now to be driven step by step, but the first step – across Europe – was just set in the 5G pioneer country of Austria, and I am pleased. All these are points that show: We want to get faster! We want to get better! We want to reform! The citizens are supposed to feel that! - That's what we're working for. I would like to thank you for participating in this small step in this reform. Thank you.

The criminals could not believe what was going on there to be ways to make maximum profit with little effort, to take the money from the people, to put them in a boat, to put a cell phone with the stored phone number of an NGO, which they should then call to push into their hands. They will then be brought to Europe, here they will only have to say asylum, and then the process will start with full care. Against this policy, which we have approved here, in particular the previous government, we take a decision – and we have to do this again. Of course, many people also experience violence on their flight. They take a risk, they make false promises – the future is not quite as rosy as it has been presented to them – and young people are beaching, and in the worst case some do not survive the crossing. Therefore, tow and trafficking in human beings must be terminated and this path must be closed across the Mediterranean Sea. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] To conclude: As has been said, it is really nice that the request has been adopted unanimously, but here too the problem arises as at the previous item on the agenda, namely that the largest opposition party is clearly saying so, but the other is doing so, because all the measures, especially those of our Minister of the Interior, are precisely to support this request that illegal migration be stopped. The lowest incomes were found at around 20,000 euros with the workers, the highest average incomes of 56 132 euros are recorded by the officials. In order to ensure that this spray can be evaluated in a correct manner, the report provides detailed information. He explains that there are a number of factors that have a positive impact on the level of income of officials. Officials are not only above average academics compared to other groups, but also, on average, significantly older than representatives of other employment groups. They are more rarely in a part-time employment relationship, and the proportion of non-permanent officials is also very low. The difference is particularly marked in men between 40 and 49 years of age. On the other hand, the proportion of women in full-time work is only 33 percent of all year round. And: Part-time workers naturally earn less than full-time workers. If the income, as in the report, is calculated per capita, this naturally expresses the statistical average and thus the average income of the groups concerned. Another word about the pensioners: We have around 2,370,000 pensioners in Austria. 89 percent of these live in Austria. After deduction of taxes and health insurance, pensioners residing in Austria received a mean annual income of 18 350 euros. ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e29905	Mr President. High house! I would like to say once again: Why does the Court of Auditors make this report? This is not a classical audit report, but it is a special task of the Court of Auditors. We do and carry out this task under the Relevant Restriction Act. We are obliged to report every two years the average income of the entire population to the National Council, the Federal Council and the Land Days, broken down by sectors, professional groups, functions, by women and men. As said, the Court of Auditors presented this report for 2016/2017 at the end of December 2018, in cooperation with Statistics Austria. We're working together because, of course, we need data sources. We make a descriptive presentation, we do not analyze the causes. To draw the conclusions from the report is, of course, yours from the facts we provide. The question of who makes it is separate from the fact that I consider it important that there is such an income report because it explains where the differences in income are, the differences in social status, the question of employment, full-time, part-time, atypical employment, self-employed, self-employed, pensioners and, of course, many other factors that play a role. We are trying to explain this income report clearly. We have made an overview leaflet on six pages, where you will immediately see, as it were, how the differences between women and men are, how the differences between the federal states are, also by sector, by education et cetera. It has already been discussed what the median income is and what the income relationship between men and women looks like. I would like to highlight one point: Of course, everything depends very much on the economic sector in which a person is self-employed. There are differences between the sectors, but also within sectors, such as the services sector. And there are links with the level of part-time and seasonal employment and with the proportion of women. I would like to highlight three sectors, such as energy supply: As a result, a very high middle income corresponds to a high full-time share and a ............................................................................................................................................................ In the area of health and social affairs, there is a relatively low average income at a minimum full-time and highest female share. And in the area of accommodation and gastronomy, there is the lowest average income with a low full-time share and a high proportion of women. I would like to end by now, because everyone can read in the report every day, not just today, but all year round, what it is hectic and what conclusions are drawn from it. In this sense, I think, this is a good basis for working for the National Council. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e30209	Mr President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) We now come to the Court of Auditors report 'Inclusive education: What is Austria's school system doing? “ It is about competence, funding, but also about a very sensitive social policy matter: how we deal with people with disabilities in education. The Court of Auditors has reviewed the Ministry of Education, the Council of State Schools in Carinthia and Tyrol, as well as the Office of the State Government in Carinthia and Tyrol, focusing on the teaching of pupils with disabilities. The aim was to review and question the actions of the Ministry for the Implementation of the United Nations Convention on Disability. A total of 40 recommendations were made by the Court of Auditors, 22 of which went directly to the Ministry of Education. Our Federal Minister has reported that eleven recommendations are in implementation, seven have already been implemented, and four recommendations from the Court of Auditors have not yet been implemented in the Ministry of Education. So, ten years ago, Austria ratified this UN Convention on the Rights of the Disabled, and the Court of Auditors also found that there were still some shortcomings as regards implementation, pace and permeability, and it was almost necessary to implement it more quickly. At that time, there was a directive on the development of inclusive model regions without special schools, in Styria, in Carinthia and in Tyrol, but only the compulsory schools, public schools and New Secondary Schools. Overall, this should have been done by 2020. There has now been a certain change of strategy or a reorientation in the new political constellation. Compliance with the timetable has not been achieved, and the Court of Auditors has recommended developing its own strategy in terms of implementation, which should include permeability up to universities and adult education. The Court of Auditors has therefore called for a qualitative improvement. What has actually been implemented? – There are three priorities for inclusive teaching, which have already been implemented. The Federal Minister has reported on this and has made it quite clear that there must be a balance between inclusive teaching and special schools. Both systems have their rights, and this is mainly about child welfare, which must always be at the heart of it. But there are also limits, and I think it is quite clear: as much inclusion as possible, but no compulsion to do so. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] What were the three concrete measures put in place here? – The Federal Ministry has provided 109 planning sites throughout Austria in order to enable a needs-based offer of best quality pedagogical support. The identification of a special educational support requirement, the choice of the appropriate In the future, educating parents on the appropriate school location will be carried out by experts of the education management itself. And the third priority is that there has been a decision-making procedure since this year on the basis of the General Administrative Procedure Act. This will increase the reliability, transparency and quality of special education promotion, and the data base will also be significantly improved. In summary, this report on the Court of Auditors has critically highlighted the issue of inclusion, but it is also absolutely clear that things are developing in the sense of our disabled people, that training should also be given the best possible quality. I think it is up to all of us to develop the inclusion here together with our Federal Minister. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-03-27-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00066_d7e30517	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Ladies and gentlemen! It was particularly impressive for me how the Carinthian educational director described these efforts, how to work to find out what fits the individual child best – how much it can be with the other children, when it needs a retreat room, how it can be cared for – and what happiness it means for the parents when they see that their child is so uplifted. But not only for parents: The Kärntner Education Director has also told us that the opportunities of disabled children who are taught inclusively are much greater on the first labour market than the opportunities of children with the same disability who have attended a special school. And the other great advantage for society as a whole is: Children who meet with disabled children in school then encounter people with disabilities quite differently as adults. It thus strengthens the interaction in society, and this is a value that goes beyond the help of the affected children and their parents, which is a value for the co-operation in society.

Conversely, however, it is also true that the federal government often does too little and supports a country federal schools. All of this comes from this intertwinedness, and we demand clarification – a clarification of responsibilities, a clarification of how we really want to meet requirements in the education system today. It is similar in the area of inclusive teaching, because it is also true in the context of inclusive teaching that many things concern school maintenance – for example, if you think of the building, accessibility, if you think of the care and assistance staff, who is legally responsible for it, but whose clarification is still in place; that is also the question of the fact that the federal government is providing special educational resources due to the financial compensation, but which may not be appropriate. This has been shown, for example, by Carinthia or Tyrol. To do this, we believe that education concepts are needed, and that it requires optimal location concepts that really allow for fair planning for individual components – according to the question of the number of students, the question of the full-day care offer, and possibly the existing building capacity. They have set up educational directives, but of course they have not yet solved the division of competence de facto. We'll take a closer look at this. It also points out that, through its participations, the universities are entering into market risks to contribute entrepreneurship, credit, credit and liability risks, which the universities are often unaware of. I have to say honestly, I am glad or happy if not so committed to the topic of school locations and school leavings, because we have experienced an unprecedented school closure wave in Styria, especially in rural areas. I do not understand that as the only criterion, the minimum number of students should be used. We had cases where new secondary schools were affected, which instead of 70 students had 64 for two or three years, and then school closures were started. There are always other criteria to be applied, such as the feasibility of the distance to the next school or the conservation status of the school, the school building. It is not very helpful if the community invests a lot of money, and then the school is closed. We are talking about the fact that in the last period of the gloriously failed reform partnership under Voves, over 40 school locations and also in the eight school locations that are now under way have been closed. It was always socialist education councillors, that was Elisabeth Grossmann, that was then Michael Schickhofer and is now Mrs. Lackner. Jörg Leichtfried knows what I'm talking about. I want to get this to the point, even we liberals in the Styria have always said, it makes no sense if there are perhaps only three or four children registered in a folk school, that is quite clear. But you should also take other criteria and not just the number of students, because the school is very important, the school is an important centrepiece for the population in a small place, for the development of society. This is also scientifically verifiable. My point of view is quite clear: Those who want to preserve the rural area, which is already being thinned and thinned, do not close schools.
